b. Replacement Service Coverage. 
The latest BDC data, on which Verizon relies, demonstrate that the Providers offer 
coverage to the locations in question.  As Chairman Carr has stated, the BDC “accurately 
detail[s] the availability of wired and wireless broadband.”134  The Network Modernization 
Order expressly endorsed use of the BDC to determine mobile wireless coverage.135  Reliance 
on the BDC is warranted given that Congress directed this granular source of coverage 
information.136  Further, Verizon’s use of 4G LTE coverage data reported in the BDC reflects a 
cautious approach, as the minimum throughput requirements of 5 Mbps downlink and 1 Mbps 
uplink far surpass the network requirements to support a mobile voice call using Voice over 
LTE.  
The Providers must certify the accuracy of their BDC submissions, which are subject to 
extensive and iterative Commission review processes.137  None of the Providers has been subject 
to a public Commission enforcement action for noncompliance with BDC requirements.  Further, 
Verizon has determined, based on publicly available information, that each of the Providers 
offers voice service.

3. 
Date of Planned Discontinuance. 
Effective on or after November 30, 2026, pending regulatory approval,153 Verizon will 
discontinue residential and business legacy voice service in the geographic areas detailed below. 
2. 
Points of Geographic Areas of Service Affected and Description of the 
Affected Service Area. 
Verizon plans to discontinue its residential and business legacy copper-based POTS 
service throughout its telephone service areas in California, Connecticut, District of Columbia, 
Illinois, Maryland, Massachusetts, Michigan, New York, Pennsylvania, and West Virginia in 
locations where (1) Verizon either offers mobile wireless service, has overbuilt fiber, or offers 
fixed wireless POTS voice replacement services that qualify as legacy POTS replacement 
services and/or (2) at least one of a select group of third parties (AT&T, T-Mobile, Xfinity, 
Spectrum, Breezeline, Mediacom, Optimum, Shentel, Sparklight, or Armstrong) offers a fiber, 
cable, or mobile wireless adequate replacement service, as described in detail herein.154 
3. 
Brief Description of Type of Service Affected. 
Verizon’s residential and business POTS services are TDM-based exchange access line 
services that consist of the serving central office line equipment and all outside plant facilities 
needed to connect the serving central office with the network interface at the demarcation point 
 
153 To the extent that additional state requirements apply to discontinuing POTS, Verizon will 
satisfy those requirements prior to effectuating the discontinuance. 
154 The telephone service areas in California, Connecticut, District of Columbia, Illinois, 
Maryland, Massachusetts, Michigan, New York, Pennsylvania, and West Virginia are depicted in 
maps in Exhibit B below.

<!-- page 109 -->
43 
of the customer premises.  The service includes optional custom calling features and the End 
User Common Line service, which allows the line to facilitate local exchange, intrastate 
interexchange, and interstate voice calling. 
4. 
Brief Description of the Dates and Method of Notice to All Affected 
Customers. 
Customer notices were sent by U.S. Mail on September 8, 2026, to customers of 
Verizon’s legacy voice service located in the discontinuance area.  Representative examples of 
the customer notices are attached as Exhibit A. 
Copies of this application are being sent via FedEx Overnight (or U.S. Priority Mail for 
P.O. Box addresses) to the governor, public utility commission, federally recognized tribes in the 
discontinuance areas, and to the Chief Information Officer of the Department of Defense, as 
required by Section 63.71(a) of the Commission’s rules.155 
5. 
Regulatory Classification of Carrier. 
Verizon offers POTS pursuant to non-dominant carrier regulation. 
6. 
Statement Identifying the Application as a Technology Transition (47 
C.F.R. § 63.602(a)(2)). 
The proposed discontinuance constitutes a technology transition under the Commission’s 
rules.  Remaining Verizon POTS customers in the discontinuance area will be required to replace 
their TDM-based voice service with a different technology or transmission medium when 
Verizon discontinues legacy voice service, as there is no other TDM-based voice service 
available to them. 
 
155 Section 63.71(a) directs applicants to submit a copy of the application to the Secretary of

Department of Defense, that position no longer exists.  We understand from a review of prior 
applications that Commission staff has advised that a copy of the application should instead be 
sent to the Department of Defense Chief Information Officer.

Attachment A - Additional Information for Home Phone Services 
Home Phone Over the Wireless Network 
These devices include battery backup capability. 
● Under typical conditions for current solutions, the battery provides up to 24 
hours of backup power during a power outage.  
● Actual battery life may vary depending on network conditions, features in use, 
call frequency and usage patterns.  
Important Notes: 
● The device does not provide line power to your home telephones. 
● Diﬀerent models may include either a rechargeable battery or replaceable AA 
batteries.  
● Replacement batteries are widely available.  
● If the device has no electrical power or battery power, it will not function, 
including for 911 emergency calls.  
Steps to take to ensure safe usage and security responsibilities: 
● You must enter the US address where you want emergency services to be sent if 
you call 911, before voice service can be activated.  If you move your equipment, 
you must update your service address with us immediately. 
● You are responsible for obtaining and maintaining a backup battery or 
rechargeable batteries for your equipment, to ensure service continuity.  
● To ensure service during an outage, a charged battery must be installed. 
● The device must be properly connected to power when not in use.  
Unlimited Digital Voice (a Frontier ėber-based service)  
These devices include battery backup capability. 
 
 
 
CMT-Frontier

<!-- page 119 -->
● Under typical conditions for current solutions, fully charged batteries in the 
battery backup unit provide 24 hours of backup power during a power outage.  
● Actual battery life may vary depending on network conditions, features in use, 
call frequency, usage patterns.  
● Replacement batteries are widely available: the backup battery unit uses 12 D 
cell batteries. 
Important Notes: 
● Unlimited Digital Voice service is delivered over ėber and does not supply power 
through the phone line. 
● Battery backup units are provided to consumers who migrate from a 
copper-based phone service, providing up to 24 hours of backup power during 
power outages. 
● If the device has no electrical power or battery power, it will not function, 
including for 911 emergency calls.   
Steps to take to ensure safe usage and security responsibilities: 
● To maintain power during a power outage, you will need a battery backup unit. 
This allows you to continue making and receiving calls, including emergency and 
911 calls.  
● If you move you must update your service address with us immediately. 
● You are responsible for obtaining and maintaining a backup battery and 
rechargeable batteries for your equipment to ensure service continuity.  
● To ensure service during an outage charged batteries must be installed. 
● You are responsible for obtaining replacement “D” cell batteries for continued 
use during an extended power outage.  
● The device must be properly connected to power when not in use.  
Landline Phones and Power Requirements 
Some phones - especially cordless models - require electricity to operate. 
● If your phone requires external power, it will not work during a power outage, 
even if your service has battery backup. 
● This includes the ability to make or receive 911 calls. 
For maximum reliability during power outages, consider using a corded phone that does 
not require external power.  
Mobile Wireless Services 
Verizon mobile wireless voice service does not provide line power to keep it operational 
during an extended electrical power outage, beyond a single charge of the mobile 
phone battery.  However, Verizon’s mobile wireless service does not require a backup 
battery to remain operational during electric power outages of several hours or less.  
Steps to take to ensure safe usage and security responsibilities: 
● To maintain phone service during these times, you will have to arrange for an 
alternative source of power for your Verizon mobile phone. 
● Modern cell phones can go without a charge for more than 24 hours when kept 
in standby mode.   
 
 
 
CMT-Frontier

<!-- page 120 -->
● For longer outages, cell phones can be recharged in a car, with a laptop, or with 
a charged power bank, which is inexpensive, typically starting at approximately 
$20, and available online and at various retail stores.   
● Cell phones will not operate without backup power in the event of an extended 
power outage. In that situation, you will not be able to make or receive calls, 
including emergency 911 calls, unless you recharge the phone with a backup 
battery. 
● Since wireless phones are mobile, they are not associated with one ėxed 
location or address.  If you make an emergency 911 call with your mobile phone, 
you should provide the emergency operator the location of your emergency and 
your wireless phone number, so if the call gets disconnected, the operator can 
call you back.  Additional information is available on the FCC’s website 
(https://www.fcc.gov/consumers/guides/wireless-911-service).

Letter of Support - Atlantic Menhaden Conservation Efforts
Multiple
10/14/26
10/20/26
■
Atlantic States Marine Fisheries Commission (ASMFC) to consider the Draft Addendum II to Amendment 3 to the
Interstate Fishery Management Plan for Atlantic Menhaden, which aims to protect critical nursery grounds in the
Chesapeake Bay by considering options to cut the industrial harvest cap by up to 50% and implement seasonal
quotas. These measures ensure enough forage fish remain in local waters to sustain vital predatory species,
supporting both regional ecological health and the local seafood and recreational fishing economies.
Draft Addendum II to Amendment 3

NEXT STEPS, IF APPROVED
Revision Date: 2FWREHU, 2026
A Traffic Safety Committee convened at 1 pm on October 15, 2026 to discuss the following topics:
1.)Speeding on Cloverdale Road - Resident request to place school zone & fines doubled signs between middle & high schools.
2.) A Blind Spot at the intersection of Oak Grove & Wheatley Road - Resident request to install an additional stop sign at the intersection
of Oak Grove Road & Wheatley Road.
3.) Parking on Bestpitch Ferry Road - Resident request to install no parking signs on both sides of Bestpitch Ferry Road from Bazzel
Church to Bestpitch Ferry Bridge.
4.) Safety Assessment- Galestown Reliance Road & Lakeshore Drive - Resident request for a safety assessment to appropriate signage,
or other measures, to increase safety
Improve traffic safety through appropriate signage, increased enforcement, and measures that promote safer driving conditions for
motorists, pedestrians, and residents.
1.) The TSC requests Council to consider adding two additional 25MPH signs in between the High & Middle schools on Cloverdale
Road as well as "Fines Doubled" signs under each MPH sign.
2.) The TSC requests Council to consider adding warning signs of some sort to alert drivers of oncoming traffic
3.) The TSC requests Council to consider placing "no parking" signs on the complainants property, 25 feet to the left of his mailbox and
25 feet to the right of his mailbox.
4.)The TSC requests that the Council consider sending letters to the Maryland State Police (MSP) and the Dorchester County Sheriff’s
Office requesting increased speed enforcement in the Galestown Reliance Road and Lakeshore Drive area.
1. Direct the Department of Public Works to coordinate the installation of the additional signage, subject to any necessary approvals.
2. Direct the Department of Public Works to evaluate appropriate warning signage and coordinate installation.
3. Direct the Department of Public Works to verify the proposed locations and coordinate installation of the no-parking signage, subject
to applicable regulations.
4. Direct Council staff to draft and distribute letters to MSP and the Dorchester County Sheriff’s Office formally requesting increased
speed enforcement in the identified area.
